# pcrec Project Plan

Working plan derived from ../../APPROACH.md. Milestones M0–M7 mirror APPROACH §9.

**CLOSE-OUT SEQUENCE IN FORCE (D125, Frank 2026-09-26): tails → active rows
(next optimization cycle HELD) → older/parked rows reassessed against
today's architecture → stop and take stock. New rows may be filed, not
scheduled ahead of their phase.** See decisions.md D125.

## Step-state format (grep'able)

Every step line matches exactly:

    - [Mx.y] STATE:<state> — <title>

States: `not-started` | `started` | `completed` | `blocked` | `deferred`

Find work:

    grep -n "STATE:started" docs/dev/plan.md
    grep -n "STATE:not-started" docs/dev/plan.md
    grep -cE "^[[:space:]]*- \[[^]]*\] STATE:not-started" docs/dev/plan.md   # ANCHORED count (81 on 2026-09-22); the unanchored grep also hits "formerly STATE:not-started" prose
    grep -c "STATE:completed" docs/dev/plan_completed.md

Completed rows are archived in docs/dev/plan_completed.md (this file keeps
zero STATE:completed rows; a completed-count grep here counts nothing but
recipe text).

Rules: update the STATE tag in place when a step changes state; expand a milestone
into substeps only when work on it begins (replace its single `[Mx.0]` line);
note blockers inline after the title with `(blocked: reason)`.

## Queue discipline (Frank, 2026-08-12)

**The BOONIES TIER sits well after the general work.** [M4-CALLOUTS],
[M4-SUBST], [V-E], [V-F], [SR-10] and their kin are PARKING SPOTS, not queue
positions: recorded so nothing is re-derived, started only after the spine —
M3 streaming, M4 captures + backtracking VM engine, M5 UTF-8, M6 feature
modules, M7 differential fuzzing — is done, unless Frank explicitly pulls
one forward. A boonies row growing substeps while spine rows sit not-started
is the smell this note exists to stop.
(Spine order re-ruled 2026-08-13: STD1 → M4 → M5 → M6 → M3 → M7 — see
"Development order" below.)

## Lane structure (Frank, 2026-08-29 — D86)

Lanes are TYPED, within the 3-concurrent ceiling: one FEATURE lane
([DD-13b]/[LIB], [DD-12]/[M5] UTF, modules), one OPTIMIZATION lane
([OPT-*], [ENG-PGO]; from the bench's outliers, measured need first),
and one ADMINISTRATIVE/STRUCTURAL lane AS NEEDED ([DD-11], [SPEC-1]
lanes, checks, doc consolidation) when a row is available and nothing
of higher priority wants the slot. A closed lane's successor comes
from the same column. Rows still open on Frank's word.

## Development order (ratified 2026-08-13)

The spine, in this order, one line of rationale each (full session rationale:
dev_journal.md 2026-08-13, sixteenth session):

- **[STD1]** — its suite re-baseline only widens with time; land it before
  anything else queues behind it.
- **M4 — captures + backtracking VM engine** — the biggest user-visible gap
  (captures) and the owner of the most parked decisions ([DD-2], [DD-7],
  [DD-9], [SR-8]).
- **M5 — UTF-8** — before M6, so feature modules are born CharSet/UTF-aware
  ([DD-12], [DD-1]) instead of being rebuilt once UTF lands.
- **M6 — PCRE feature modules** — lookaround, backrefs, atomic groups,
  named-groups, conditionals, recursion. The assertions module is the one
  M6 piece that needs no VM — a flexible slot, schedulable whenever a lane
  is free.
- **M3 — streaming input** — moved to last-but-one because its API must be
  designed once against the FINAL engine+feature set ([OS-3]'s evidence —
  lookbehind/backref window retention); building it earlier would mean
  rebuilding it.
- **M7 — hardening** — differential fuzzing was already pulled forward (to
  M2); the residue is testdata import and the freestanding/embedded
  profile.

Sequencing notes: the backrefs / atomic-groups / substitution-template
design notes (under M4 below) should be WRITTEN BEFORE M4 starts — they are
M4's design customers, and the substitution template compiler is
matcher-independent (Frank, 2026-08-12), so its design note need not wait
on M4 either. After M7, [BENCH-1] builds the feature-spanning benchmark +
prioritizer; then the OPT waves open, [MECH-3] first, with the prioritizer
setting the work order.

Design notes moved here from [MOD-0.1]'s archived entry (docs/dev/plan_completed.md),
2026-08-13 — M4's design customers, per the Development order above:

**DESIGN NOTE FOR `backrefs` (Frank, 2026-08-12 tenth session): the
engines column's blanket VM_ONLY on the digit rows is provisional and
splits under an AOT compiler.** At match time a backreference is a string
compare against the group's captured text — which is exactly what the
backtracking VM will do, and what the DFA engine cannot (subset
construction erases thread identity; no execution point knows a capture,
and `(a*)b\1`'s state would need unbounded text — the pumping-lemma
classic). But when the referenced group's language is FINITE, the backref
is REGULAR and compiles away statically: `(abc)\1` is `abcabc`, `(a|b)\1`
is `aa|bb` — expand each choice with the reference synchronized, pure
DFA, zero runtime cost, and only an ahead-of-time compiler can afford the
expansion (bounded by the existing NFA/DFA caps and gcc-compile-time
budgets; infinite-language groups keep the VM). So the module's engine
answer is per-PATTERN, not per-row: finite-group backrefs → ENGM_DFA via
expansion, infinite-group → ENGM_VM. The `engines` column stays design
intent until then (nothing consumes it before SR-8/M4); do not read the
rows' VM_ONLY as a measured limit.

**SAME-SESSION COMPANION NOTE for `atomic-groups`/possessives (Frank):
the (?> row's VM_ONLY splits the same way, and the naive intuition is
BACKWARDS twice over.** A DFA never backtracks in the first place —
subset construction keeps every alternative alive, which is exactly the
NON-possessive semantics — so `a*+` is not a free annotation: it CHANGES
the language (`a*+ab` matches nothing, `a*ab` matches "aab"), and a
naively-determinized atomic group silently implements the wrong one.
But the language stays REGULAR (atomic/possessive are CUT operators;
Berglund et al., "Cuts in Regular Expressions" — cuts preserve
regularity with possibly-exponential conversion), so pure-DFA
compilation is achievable, and the construction's one primitive —
determine the sub-expression's OWN priority-first match endpoint online,
ignoring the continuation — is precisely the priority accept-pruning
pcrec's subset construction is already built around. Blowup bounded by
the existing caps; the disjoint-follow special case (PCRE2's own
auto-possessification direction, a*b ≡ a*+b when nothing that follows
can start with an `a`) is free in both directions. Engine answer again
per-PATTERN: cut-constructible → ENGM_DFA, else VM.

## M5 — UTF-8

- [UTF-PAT] STATE:not-started (CHARTERED by Frank 2026-09-05, fifty-fifth session, UNSCHEDULED: "using utf as the Regex encoding. i'm guessing its close but needs validation and testing/support") — UTF-8 AS THE PATTERN-TEXT ENCODING: the contract, validated and tested. Frank's guess measured CORRECT at chartering: stage 2's pattern-char decode already handles raw UTF-8 pattern text under `-e utf8` — a raw `[α-ω]` (bytes 5B CE B1 2D CF 89 5D) emits the IDENTICAL machine to `[\x{3b1}-\x{3c9}]` (manager probe, artifact diff: only the provenance stamp/pattern-comment lines differ). What the row owes is everything AROUND that: (1) the CONTRACT stated in docs/spec/ (cli.md/registry.md): pattern text is interpreted in the PATTERN's encoding = the `-e` encoding (byte: pattern bytes are bytes — a raw `[α-ω]` under byte is legitimately the byte-class reading; utf8: pattern bytes decode to characters), including what an ILL-FORMED pattern byte sequence does (expect: clean compile error naming the offset, never a silent byte reading — MEASURE first, today's behavior unverified); (2) a VALIDATION SWEEP: raw-vs-\x{} artifact equivalence over a generated population (literals, classes, ranges, negation, case boundaries at 0x7F/0x80, astral, combining marks in literals), oracle-verified against 10.46 compiled WITH PCRE2_UTF on the pattern; (3) a D27-style corpus arm in tests/utf8/ (raw-spelling twins of existing blocks; the .rxt format's own file-encoding question — a `pattern` line carrying raw UTF-8 — is the manager's call per dd13b); (4) provenance surfaces: the pattern-comment/rx_info.pattern render for non-ASCII pattern text (today it renders \xNN-escaped — probably right, but say so in the spec); (5) registry/compliance rows if any construct's answer changes by pattern encoding. NOT this row: UCP semantics for \w/\d (stage 3-4 territory), utf8-by-default (a separate ruling if ever). Sonnet-sized measurement+corpus+spec; engine changes only if (1)'s measurement finds a silent-byte hole (then it is a miscompile-class fix, escalate).
- [K50-BNDSTART] archived to plan_completed.md (completed 2026-09-06 — merged 8e0fe77f, abi 24, K50 fixed on all three mechanisms + the site-2 second face; D97 ratified PCREC_ERR_STARTPOS; Linux battery owed at next slot)
- [UTF-RW] STATE:not-started (CHARTERED by Frank 2026-09-05, fifty-fifth session, SEQUENCED after the utf build (M5.0 stages 3-5) lands: "go out and find some good real world utf regex for testing. This will be foreign (for me) language stuff and I don't want the English bias built into the system to cause us to miss solid use case") — THE REAL-WORLD UTF REGEX HARVEST + THE CLASS-SHAPE CENSUS. Two deliverables from one collection: (1) THE HARVEST — sourced real-world UTF-8 patterns from NON-ENGLISH-LOCALE ecosystems, deliberately outside this project's own authoring bias: validation patterns from CJK/Arabic/Devanagari/Thai/Cyrillic/Greek/Hebrew codebases and frameworks (form validators, ID/postal formats, tokenizers, log/line parsers written BY speakers of those languages, not English-locale approximations), i18n test suites (W3C/CLDR-adjacent), combining-mark and RTL material — every pattern with PROVENANCE AND LICENSE recorded (REFERENCES.md-style citation discipline; no unlicensed corpus text lands in the tree), oracle-verified against 10.46 under PCRE2_UTF, landed as a tests/utf8/ corpus arm (not D27-blinded — a harvest is public material, the blinding lever does not apply; the D27 lever for this milestone already ran) AND proposed to the bench as a real-world utf SET (their syntax-census/[B36] machinery is the natural consumer — coordinate via the channel). (2) THE CLASS-SHAPE CENSUS over the harvest, which is the row's measurement payload (Frank, same day: real classes may hold MANY NON-CONTIGUOUS characters — "degenerate cases... which would need to be if-then'd. Perhaps a strategy is bitmaps over clusters. Needs real world examples"): per-pattern distributions of class interval counts, per-LEAD-BYTE cluster counts, lowered branch counts, VM chain lengths and DFA state counts, split by script family — the population that decides whether Frank's CLUSTER-BITMAP candidate (per-lead-byte continuation-set bitmaps — a 2-byte character's continuation space is 64 values = one 8-byte mask per cluster, replacing a chain of range tests in the VM lowering) is a real form-ladder rung or a shape real patterns never produce. D77 discipline: the candidate is NAMED here and BUILT only on the census's numbers. Standing evidence to weigh, not assume: the DFA side already cluster-shares aggressively (\p{L} = 648 intervals minimizes to 283 states — the byte-class equivalence map is doing the collapsing; the hazard is the VM route's linear branch chain, and any fix is a [FORM-CHAR]-family form question); form_char_step0's family-B result (byte-tier bitmap tables LOSE .text vs range compares on sparse ASCII classes) does NOT transfer unexamined — a continuation-cluster mask is a different size/shape trade at a different alphabet width. Sequencing note: the harvest wants stages 3-4 compiled (\p, Unicode fold — most real i18n patterns use them); a class/literal-only SUBSET is compilable at stage 2 and may be collected early if a lane is idle. Sonnet-sized collection + census; any resulting form rung is its own measured engine row.
- [UTF-EXTENT] STATE:not-started (FILED 2026-09-30 by lane uvbuild at [UTF-VALID]'s build, per D133 Q2: "`extent` recorded, NOT built") — THE SECOND VALUE OF THE `-futf-check` CONTRACT AXIS, "check as you move forward": after the engine answers, refuse iff an ill-formed sequence begins in `[f, e]` (e = the match end, inclusive; `[f, n)` on no match). Contract, cost (scratch caps + a wrapper split of the DFA entry bodies, `e` routed out) and the loop-level equivalence with PCRE2 are in docs/design/utf_valid_design.md §2.2; the spelling `-futf-check=extent` is RESERVED and refused by name today (cli/main.c). lands when that inliner lands; payoff on the DEFAULT build is the hybrid prefilter coming back ON (no A_BREF in the tree; today fit.prefilter = false for every backref pattern); (b) shares its definitely-set analysis with this predicate — one pass; (d) is the only genuinely backref-local item and is dropped unless a measurement asks; none blocks the close)

- [EMIT-ENTRIES] STATE:not-started (CHARTERED by Frank 2026-09-21: "emit control for the functions produced (search, match). if X depends on Y and only X is emitted, then Y can be emitted statically") — ENTRY-POINT EMIT CONTROL. Today every artifact exports the five `<prefix>`-scoped entries unconditionally (docs/spec/match_api.md:467; `--no-captures` is the only subtractive lever and it selects an engine shape, not an entry set). The row: a caller names the entry set it wants (a flag, a `.rxt` `target` attribute, or both — the manager designs, Frank rules); an entry not named is emitted `static` when another named entry depends on it (the un-suffixed entries forward to the `_in` twins, match_api.md:188 — so naming `_search` alone makes `_search_in` static and drops `_match*` entirely), and not emitted at all when nothing named needs it. What it buys: smaller artifacts and no exported symbol a consumer did not ask for (the D104 hazard class). Contract: the `rx_info.nentries`/`search_form` stamps already exist to describe the set — the spec hunk says what a reduced set stamps. abi event by construction (D76). Design gate before code (the four lenses, memory `pcrec-design-evaluation-lenses`): is the entry set a per-target attribute (source-file world, D118) or a per-invocation flag, and how the harness's identity gates treat a reduced artifact.

## Beyond M7 — long-term vision (Frank, 2026-08-09)

Direction, not scheduled work. Recorded so the architecture is not painted into
a corner that would make these expensive later. Each becomes a milestone only
after the current ladder is complete and the result is something we are happy
with.

**POSITIONING NOTE (Frank + manager, 2026-08-13 sixteenth session).** The
niche, in one sentence: *PCRE2 semantics, compiled to verified,
dependency-free C — fastest where the pattern is known ahead of time, and
the only one that does compiled substitution.* Its elements, each with an
owner: (1) PERFORMANCE, honestly scoped — an AOT compiler wins where the
compiler saw the exact pattern (specialized single-pattern scanning,
startup, latency), and does not chase Hyperscan-scale SIMD multi-pattern;
[BENCH-1]'s prioritizer maps both the worst cells (fix) and the best cells
(advertise). (2) EMBEDDED / NO-LIBRARY — the defensible core. The incumbent
to name is re2c; differentiation is PCRE2 syntax+semantics, leftmost-first,
the future captures/backrefs tier, and the verification story. What the
code already guarantees is the sales sheet: all-const tables (TS-1,
enforced), no malloc/errno/locale in generated code, thread-safe by
construction, deterministic — certifiable vendored source. M7's
freestanding profile cashes this. (3) THE VERIFICATION STORY IS A PRODUCT
FEATURE — "differentially verified against libpcre2; unsupported syntax
fails loudly, never miscompiles" is a claim no neighbor in this niche
makes. (4) LATENCY — time-to-first-match from process start (~zero for us:
tables page in from .rodata; PCRE2 pays pcre2_compile + JIT warmup every
process) and short-subject per-call overhead, the dimension real workloads
(log lines, field validation) are dominated by and benchmarks skip —
measured under [BENCH-1]. (5) COMPILED SUBSTITUTION as the headline
differentiator — see [M4-SUBST]'s beyond-PCRE2 direction. Developer-
experience directions that serve the niche are the [V-*] rows below,
including V-G/V-H (added this session).

    - [OPT-REVEND] STATE:not-started (under [OPTLOOP] candidates per D137; filed 2026-10-05 at Frank's question; census docs/dev/optloop/revend_census.md, lane revend) — REVERSE-FROM-END SEARCH: for a pattern every match of which ends at `n` (`\z`) or at `n`/`n-1` (`$`, `\Z`), seed the existing reverse DFA at `n` (and at `n-1` when `s[n-1]=='\n'`), run it backward until it dies or reaches `search_from`, and take the smallest accepting position `s*` as the match START; then run the artifact's existing anchored entry once from `s*` for the end (leftmost-first can still choose `n` or `n-1`) and the captures (the VM over the found span). This REPLACES today's O(subject) forward pass (an end-pinned machine cannot stop before `n`; the skip loop helps only while parked in state 0) by O(match) work, on matching AND non-matching subjects. EXACTNESS: every match ends in {n, n-1}, so the starts of all matches are the starts of matches ending there and `s*` is the leftmost start (the same argument the shipped forward-then-reverse protocol uses at the forward end); the end is never read off the reverse walk. WHERE IT DOES NOT HELP: start-anchored `^...$` (class S: one attempt at 0, D77's `(?:P)\z` whole-subject idiom), bounded width on byte encodings ([OPT-ENDWIN] already clamps the start window, 24 ns on 1 MiB; the D77 "final-byte skip gap" is moot there), `(?m)$` (pins nothing to the end, D62 control 3), VM-only artifacts (no reverse machine), `\G`, a trailing lookaround, and a leading unbounded repeat whose run IS the subject (`.*x$` on one huge line: no worse, no better). Under utf8 [OPT-ENDWIN] declines (K49/K50); a reverse walk is the natural replacement and must discharge the same boundary obligation. D77 LINEAGE: D77 declined an end-anchored generation AXIS and named the gap "a FOLD ON THE IDIOM (the skip loop reasoning about `\z`)"; [OPT-ENDWIN] built that fold for the bounded half; this is the UNBOUNDED half, a different mechanism (a seeded walk, not a clamp), recorded under [OS-4]'s measure-the-split charter. CENSUS (4,857 patterns, 345 bench + 4,512 corpus blocks, instrument cross-checked 0/0 against the shipped end_window fact): 22 bench byte + 4 utf8 and 733 corpus rows are end-pinned; the bench's are 17 start-anchored + 5 bounded-width (all at the floor, 24-454 ns vs `jit` 51 us-1.2 ms) and **ZERO start-unanchored unbounded**; the corpus has 33 distinct (47+1 rows, 32 DFA-routable), all anchors/view correctness witnesses. Scratch-tier Linux: today's linear scan on the idiom costs `\d+$` 0.87, `\w+$` 3.15, `\s+$` 2.5-3.1, `[a-z]+\.txt$` 3.2 ns/B (0.9-3.4 ms per MiB) against 40-160 ns for the same shapes bounded and windowed. TRIGGER (D77, not met): a bench cell in this population — an `anc-tail` family (`\d+$`, `\s+$`, `\w+\z`, `[^/]+$`, `.*\.txt$`; matching and non-matching tails on a long subject) showing pcrec-vs-peer at rank — or a user/real-world pattern census naming the idiom (the bench's `wild-*` validators are all `^...$`). First owed step when it fires: the hand-twin (replace the emitted forward loop by the seeded reverse loop) with answer-identity over the `end_window.rxt` subjects.

A third kit sink on `mf_use`, a FUNCTION-ENTRY point pcrec names at the top of the entry body, for setup that is invariant to a pcrec-owned OUTER loop but not a compile-time constant (e.g. a value from the subject length or end pointer, or an alignment prologue). Candidate sites: the pre-check in the attempt loop, find-all retry, the hybrid re-seed. Today the kit has only file scope (`mf_define`, `mf_flush_helpers`, `static const` tables) and the use point. Pattern-constant setup (`set1` of a literal, mask loads) was first assumed to need neither ("gcc hoists it or it is a constant"); CORRECTED 2026-10-08 (kit r9fu measurement, lane/memfn-r9 @ fbc4a410): gcc 15.2 rebuilds set1 broadcasts at every function entry, even from a `static const`, so entry setup is real per-call cost; the only candidate site today is the filed OFS run-pinned form. RULE (Frank): build SIMD forms WITHOUT it, then measure whether adding it moves the needle. TRIGGER (D77): a SIMD form whose assembly shows per-call setup re-done inside an outer loop, plus a measured gain from a hand twin that hoists it, under D147 addendum 13's bar.     - [MEMFN-RTDISPATCH] STATE not-started — FILED, NOT SCHEDULED (Frank, 2026-10-08, D155). RUNTIME DISPATCH for SIMD forms. Why: a 500 KB artifact must not need 4-5 whole copies, one per CPU. OUT OF SCOPE: per-arch separate artifacts (one lib.so per -march, selected at load) already work through static selection; pcrec needs nothing for them. SHAPE: per-site multiversioning — only the hot helpers are duplicated per level; the level set is named at compile time and need not cascade (e.g. "AVX-512 or scalar"); static and runtime selection share the same file-scope helpers (D155 Q-R9-6 amendment). DISPATCH BY FREQUENCY CLASS per site: INFREQUENT sites (pre-check, find-start; about once per search call) may choose at runtime; FREQUENT hot-loop sites never pay a hardware check — they take a static choice, the lowest common level of the selected set or a named most-common level. Each site states its class (e.g. PRE FUNC per call; the OFS re-seed sits inside the scan loop). TRIGGER (D77): more than one architecture selected for runtime support AND a hot-loop SIMD form exists — possibly theoretical today. If built, the class is a `freq` column in DELEG_SITES (D155 addendum 1, Q-R9-11). Text: memfn/docs/responses.md (lane/memfn-m7 tip).

- [FIND-MIX] STATE:not-started (FILED by Frank 2026-09-28, eighty-fourth session, D125: filed, UNSCHEDULED; pairs with [FIND-UTF8-DEFAULT]) — WEIGHTED MIXTURE of findings data. Witness: JSON subjects carrying Russian strings. json-only leaves Cyrillic at the zero floor; russian-only ranks JSON structure (" : {) as RARE — worse than no data; today's `include` composes PER KIND by shadowing (first block along the chain answers), never by addition, so json + a russian cpfreq answers every -e utf8 question from the russian table alone. The right composition: rate = w·A + (1−w)·B, w = the subject's share of each source; w only matters on keys BOTH sources carry. Counts are the stored form (lossless, additive merge — design §0.1/R3-R4), so it is scale-then-add. BUILD ORDER: analyzer-side first (`pcrec-analyze --merge A:w B:(1−w)` emitting an ordinary bundle; no compiler change); an in-bundle `mix` line only on a measured need (D77). An exemplar analysis is the same thing with a MEASURED w. Distinct from [FINDINGS-SELINC] (a filter on which kinds a link contributes, not a mixture).

Motivating case: K66's witness `(x?)([a-z]+)+eeeeeeee~#~#~#~#\1` — find the necessary inner literal L, then go outward from it. Split the pattern as P·L·S. Three tactics on file, weakest precondition first: (a) SEED — start VM attempts at L's found position minus L's offset bound (= [OPT-VMSEED], plan row above; today the no-DFA-front pre-check discards the found position, src/gen/emit_dfa.c:810-841, emit_vm.c:12731); (b) REVERSE-DFA START — reverse DFA over P from each L occurrence gives the exact start set, then the forward VM runs the whole match from there (needs P backref-free; S may hold backrefs; Rust-regex "reverse inner", APPROACH.md:1032's lead); (c) REVERSE-THEN-SKIP (Frank's shape) — reverse over P, then resume FORWARD from L's end over S only, re-scanning nothing. Exact only when: the split is unambiguous (P cannot consume/overlap L — the witness breaks this: [a-z]+ eats e's), the reverse parse of P yields PCRE's forward-priority captures (nocaps, or captures unambiguous; required too when S back-references P's groups), and leftmost-start order is preserved across L occurrences. Also on file: why backref patterns get NO DFA prefilter at all — a superset DFA with \N relaxed to any-string (the count-collapsed superset trick) would be a sound prefilter (unverified; a question, not a claim). Rejected on file: a true backwards-running backtracking VM (a reversed pass meets \N before its group). Planning principle (Frank: arbitrary patterns make the tactical plan tricky): no general planner — a small fixed set of tactic ROWS, each with a precondition computed from [PATFACTS] facts and a one-time soundness argument, first-match, plain VM as the always-sound last row. FIRST STEP when opened: a TACTIC CENSUS over the VM-only-at-auto population (backref/linked-call) + --engine=vm, reporting which of (a)/(b)/(c)'s preconditions hold per pattern (D77: build only what measurably pays). DELIVERED + MERGED 2026-09-23 ~10:1x (lane vardesign): four opinions — (1) var-bearing artifacts' EXISTING entries gain a trailing `const rx_var *vars` parameter (no rx_bind, no bound state — match_api §5.3/§10.5); (2) replace = D38's one function with out==NULL sizing (subst_template_design.md is already ruled wholesale by D38 — replace_design.md adds only the variable layer); (3) TWO states, UNSET and EMPTY (bash's `:-` vs `-`): an UNSET at a bare `${name}` in a PATTERN refuses the call, in a REPLACEMENT renders empty (D38 Q3); (4) caseless VIABLE NOW via the mechanism itself (a pattern variable IS a backreference with a caller-supplied span — vm_bref — so the caseless compare is the backref's own; the preprocessing proposal declined: a fold is a relation, not a string). MVP: the shared expansion engine with `${name}` + `:-`/`:+` reaching a VM-route pattern variable (caseless included) and first/global replace. - [V-A] STATE:not-started — PCRE2 compatibility layer: a drop-in surface for callers who already speak PCRE2, so adopting pcrec does not mean rewriting call sites. Interacts with DD-3 (generated-API versioning) — a compat layer is a second consumer of the generated contract. TWO surfaces (Frank, 2026-08-12): the PCRE2-native API, and a POSIX `regex.h` shim (regcomp/regexec/regfree, à la pcre2posix) — a smaller surface with wider adoption reach, since decades of C code speaks regex.h and never touched PCRE2
- [V-B] STATE:not-started — usage libraries for other languages: bindings over the generated C. Note the generated code already has no runtime dependency on pcrec, which is what makes this cheap; keep it that way
- [V-C] STATE:not-started — a grep CLI built on pcrec, the natural end-user demonstration that the speed mandate (D18) actually shows up in a real tool
- [V-D] STATE:not-started (CROSS-NOTE 2026-08-19: [DD-11]'s definitions architecture makes this row cheaper than its design note assumed — a flavour = front end + BINDING LIBRARY over the reduced core; but read DD-11 note (h)'s honest limit: POSIX leftmost-LONGEST is core preference semantics, not a binding) — translators from other regex syntaxes into the base tier: grep/egrep (BRE/ERE), python `re`, and PCRE2-flavour differences. Pairs with V-C (a grep CLI needs BRE/ERE) and with V-A. Design note: these are FRONT-END modules that lower into the existing AST, exactly the shape APPROACH §3's parser extension points already anticipate — no engine work, which is what makes the direction affordable
- [LIB] STATE:not-started — SUBPATTERN LIBRARIES (RULED 2026-08-28, Frank: "depends on rxt format" — the row BLOCKS on [DD-13b], the .rxt format half; the spine question is not asked again until that lands. Frank, 2026-08-24, thirty-ninth session; PLANNED — its place relative to the spine is NOT ruled (Frank ~22:0x: "planned but I don't know that I'd put them on the spine"); to be discussed at [DD-14]'s close): (1) the .rxt format grows so a file can carry MULTIPLE patterns that reference each other through SUBROUTINE CALLS (definitions + patterns in one file, the way [DD-14] is being built); (2) the same mechanism user-facing — a user INCLUDES a library of named subpatterns and calls them from a pattern by name; (3) pcrec SHIPS a LIBRARY STORE of tested subpatterns (email address, IP address, quoted string, HTML pieces, …). Relations: [DD-11] (scope-resolved definitions whose values are inserted — this row is that architecture's user-facing form; a library is a definitions scope), [V-E] (multi-pattern compilation units — the unit boundary), [DD-14.G] (splice + dead-capture elision is what makes a library subpattern cost NOTHING: the artifact byte-identical to the hand-inlined pattern — the email specimen's bar), [DD-14.F] (the DEFINE row is the in-pattern spelling of a definition). Design points to settle at charter: NAME RESOLUTION (pattern's own groups first, then included libraries in order; collisions refused by name; PCRE2 has no include, so this is pcrec's compile-time namespace — the oracle sees the EXPANDED pattern, which makes the harness's expansion the SPLICE-vs-LINKAGE `A == B` control by construction); the .rxt directives (`define name <pattern>` / `lib <file>` per file or block; the harness expands for libpcre2 and passes the library to pcrec); the API surface (`pcrec_options` gains a definitions input; CLI `--lib FILE`; versioning per [DD-3]); the STORE's discipline (each entry oracle-verified, a D27-blinded author writes its corpus, pcrec-bench carries it as a benchmark row; the RFC 5322 email specimen is entry #1). Depends on [DD-14] closing (G in particular). Not started unprompted; the charter conversation opens at [DD-14]'s close with the post-spine direction note (journal 2026-08-24).
- [V-E] STATE:not-started (D88 2026-08-29: ONE artifact per emitted file, always — an emission set is a DIRECTORY of per-artifact files + a manifest header, composition is LINKING; no multi-pattern TU; R-VE-3's cross-artifact table sharing is link-level, deferred D77) — MULTI-PATTERN COMPILATION UNITS and the DESIGN INPUT (Frank, 2026-08-26 ~21:1x, recorded, NOT chartered — no new effort at 95 % of the subscription): make `struct rx_info` the IDENTITY of a pattern in a multi-pattern unit, not the symbol prefix — (1) a nested struct of ENTRY-POINT function pointers (search / match / match_caps / the `_in` variants / info) in rx_info — the external entry surface only; D82's per-machine inline accessors (step/dead/accept) never go behind a pointer; a direct-call caller keeps the named symbol; (2) the user-facing NAME (today's prefix) + the pattern source in rx_info, so the emitter DERIVES unique C symbols per pattern and nobody types prefixes beyond a few patterns; (3) one unit-level `const struct rx_info *const <unit>_patterns[]` + count, so a caller finds an implementation by ANY rx_info field (name, source, engine, ncaps…) with a linear scan and an emitted 10-line helper (generated code stays dependency-free). Consequences: an abi bump (append at the end, the [DD-13c] shape); the first consumer is pcrec-bench's shim (40 `pb_*` accessors over rx_info today) at its first multi-pattern sub-bench — D77's trigger for the runtime surface. Two additions (Frank, ~21:2x): (4) emit the unit-level array SORTED BY NAME so lookup by name is a binary search (the emitter knows every name at generation time; the sort order is a stated contract and a check reads it off the artifact); (5) the whole surface — entry-point pointers, name, the unit array and its helper — is an OPTIONAL emit ([EMIT-SET]'s shape: a caller who only uses direct symbol calls pays nothing for a table it never reads; the deny flag masks it out of the file, answer-identity unaffected). ADDENDUM 2026-09-08 (fifty-seventh session): Frank RE-PROPOSED this independently ("an optional rx organizer — an array of rx_info structs that can be searched"), confirming the direction; two facts moved since recording — `rx_info.name`/`nentries` LANDED ([DD-13b.W1.2]), so item (2)'s identity half exists, and [TT-4M] (darwin batched-build validation) became a SECOND candidate consumer: its step-1 dispatch main() is a hand-rolled degenerate organizer, and step 2 (harness adoption), if the numbers open it, should consume THIS row's unit array instead of keeping a test-only dispatcher (general-mechanisms rule; implement-then-replace fine). D77 trigger unchanged: the first real consumer (bench shim's first multi-pattern sub-bench, or TT-4M step 2) opens the design pass. DESIGN INPUTS (Frank, 2026-09-08 evening, recorded for the design pass, NOT ruled): (a) ERROR ATTRIBUTION is a first-class requirement of any batched build — per-pattern builds attribute trivially; an .rxt-level build must attribute every error to its block (a future multi-pattern pcrec mode's diagnostic contract is compiler-like: `<file>.rxt:<line>: pattern '<name>': ...`, all errors not first-stops); D88's one-artifact-per-TU already makes GCC-side attribution free (the diagnostic names the file = the pattern), and one gcc process over N TUs keeps that while batching the spawn — the failing-member recovery is drop-the-named-TU-and-relink ([TT-4M] deliverable 4 prices it). (b) THE ORGANIZER IS A SINGLE UNIT REGARDLESS OF BUILD SHAPE — it must include every member however many .rxt files/targets contributed. Two candidate mechanisms for the design pass: the emitted SORTED array (item 4 above, binary search, needs one generation point that sees all members = the composer) vs GNU LINKER-SECTION self-registration (each artifact plants its rx_info pointer in a named section, the organizer walks __start_/__stop_ — link-composed, no central generation point, unsorted so linear scan or sort-at-init; gcc-dialect in-bounds). (c) SEVERAL INDEPENDENT SETS are wanted — a set is a link-time grouping (section name or array per set carries the set name); open sub-question: set membership declared in the .rxt (a target property — the lean, targets already live there) vs at emission (a flag).
- [ART-MGR] STATE:not-started (CHARTERED as DESIGN-ONLY by Frank 2026-09-30, eighty-seventh session: aspirational new functionality, "nothing concrete yet, a direction document to refine over time"; placed in Beyond M7 beside its siblings because it is direction that would become a milestone, not a one-off research item) — THE ARTIFACT MANAGER: organizing MANY pattern artifacts in one program. Frank's items: (1) STATIC — an array of rx_info plus helper scripts, rx_info carrying function links to every user entry; (2) DYNAMIC — load .so files as needed; (3) compile-on-demand, a quasi-JIT; (4) unload; (5) a dynamic file cache; (6) a new runtime library + pcrec changes + a CLI consumer (a `pcrecgrep`?); (7) finder tools, static and dynamic. DESIGN DOC: docs/design/artifact_manager.md (lane artmgr) — requirements R1-R8 traced to those items, the layering (L0/L1 generated and dependency-free = [V-E]'s rx_info entry-pointer struct and sorted catalog; L2 a SEPARATE hosted loader library that artifacts never reference; L3 pcrec changes incl. a pcrec-published cache key; L4 CLI; L5 finders), hazards (K80 is L1's prerequisite; K79 blocks a canonical cache prefix; RTLD_GLOBAL default on macOS; D19 — the loader is the first mutable-state component; unload by refcounted handles; the cache dir executes code, and compile-on-demand makes the emitter's pattern escaping an injection boundary; freestanding bounds L0/L1), stages S1-S7 each with its D77 trigger, and nine open questions with recommendations (§9). FIRST STEP = S1, [V-E] item (1) (a counted `const struct rx_entries *` of the five prefix-independent entries, plus `prefix` and `version`, appended to rx_info as one abi event), gated on [V-E]'s own trigger (the bench shim's first multi-pattern sub-bench, or retiring dispatch_gen.sh's switch). Cross-references: [V-E] builds L0/L1 and stays the row that does ([ART-MGR] adds L2-L5 on top, never re-charters it); [EMIT-SET] (the surface's optional emission); [MULTI-PAT] (a fused pattern set is ONE catalog member); [LIB] (the word 'library' stays its); [FREESTANDING]; [PFX-1]; [DD-3] (the loader's abi forward-compat policy). Terms ruled provisional in the doc: CATALOG / LOADER / CACHE (not set/unit/registry/store). Not started unprompted; nothing is built before its stage's trigger. **ISA SELECTION CROSS-NOTE (2026-10-04, [MEMFN] R1c, docs/design/memfn/isa_evaluation.md §5):** the L1 catalog (S2) and L2 loader (S3/S4) are the recommended place to pick among per-ISA VARIANTS of one request (Frank's "(d) multi-artifact selection"): pcrec emits N declared-ISA artifacts (psABI v1/v3/v4, v1 mandatory), each stamped in rx_info; L1 = a composer-emitted variant group + a pure first-match `<catalog>_pick(group, level)` + a baseline `<catalog>_cpu_level()`, the CALLER holding the result (L1 is const); L2 picks before dlopen. Hazard: static-catalog variants must NOT carry GNU_PROPERTY_X86_ISA_1_NEEDED (OR-merged across the link). Depends on S1 (rx_entries) and [MEMFN]'s A3 stamp; K79/K80 discharged. D77 trigger: a measured level gain on ubuntubudu (isa_evaluation.md §3.3 L-2/L-1). Questions Q7-Q11 open to Frank.
  CROSS-PATTERN FINDER (Frank, 2026-08-12; boonies tier by his word —
  recorded now, built with a customer). N named regexes into ONE emitted
  file: per-pattern named entry points exactly as today (a statically-known
  caller pays no dispatch — D20's rule holds), SHARED DATA deduplicated by
  CONTENT (the driver is M5: a dozen patterns each carrying a private copy
  of the unicode tables adds up; share by content hash, so sharing is only
  ever dedup of identical bytes and never forces a pattern's specialized
  table into a common shape), and OS-0's finder generalized by ONE AXIS:
  D20's selector already dispatches over option-combinations of one
  pattern; the same interface selects the PATTERN too. **This is OS-0's
  candidate FIRST CUSTOMER** — the finder was deferred for lack of one.
  D20's two structural properties still bind: dispatch resolves once per
  call and never reaches the hot loop; a single-pattern single-option
  request emits byte-for-byte today's output. Usage modes to design BEFORE
  building (Frank: "we should think about how it's used"): CLI
  multi-pattern args with per-pattern names, and a manifest file for build
  integration ([V-F] is the third consumer). NOTE (Frank, 2026-08-13):
  this row also answers two use-case questions from the positioning
  discussion — the system for ORGANIZING/FINDING the regex functions you
  have compiled is this row's finder + named entry points, and the
  MANIFEST is the user-facing FILE FORMAT for specifying regexes (name,
  pattern, flags, encoding per entry → one compiled unit); design the
  manifest as a first-class user surface, not a build artifact.
  AMENDED 2026-08-13 (Frank, composition discussion; syntax and semantic
  choices TBD, his to rule): the manifest gains NAMED DEFINITIONS with
  CROSS-REFERENCES — `regexa = abc|def` usable inside a later
  `regexb = xyz(<regexa>)*` — making the file a module system for
  regexes, built up in steps. TWO COMPOSITION TIERS, kept distinct:
  SOURCE-LEVEL (manifest references, AST-inlined at compile time — zero
  runtime cost, DFA compilability preserved, the default) and LINK-LEVEL
  (M4-CALLOUTS' aligned ABI, for separately-compiled parts and
  non-regex predicates). OPEN CHOICE (Frank's, Q2/K4-tier — measured,
  never read from docs): PCRE2 spelling via (?(DEFINE))/(?&name) desugar
  — composed pattern is valid PCRE2, libpcre2 becomes the oracle for the
  composed form, but subroutine-call semantics are ATOMIC and shift
  capture numbering — vs own reference spelling with plain inlining
  semantics (beyond-PCRE2, clean namespace, same shape as M4-SUBST's
  ruling). Reference CYCLES are rejected with a clean diagnostic (true
  recursion is non-regular; a future VM-side module's business, never
  inlining's). Positioning parity note: re2c and lex/flex both ship
  named-definition composition — established practice in exactly our
  claimed niche; PCRE2 semantics on top is the differentiator.
  AMENDED 2026-08-14 (D39.2, `docs/dev/decisions.md`): rx-reference group
  numbering is APPENDED — the primary keeps its own 1..N stable; each
  inserted regex's groups append at N+1.. in insertion order; names are
  kept, and D39.1's exported name→number index is the lookup path.
  Backrefs inside an inserted regex renumber to their appended positions
  at insert time (compile-time). The name-collision policy RESOLVED by
  the D39 addendum: caller-supplied LABELED REFERENCES per insertion
  ("a:reg1", "b:reg1"), stored as the index's `ref` column; nested
  insertions compose a path ("c:a"). Still open, ruled at V-E design
  time: path spelling/separator, label mandatory-vs-optional for single
  insertions, and lookup-key semantics (name-alone when unambiguous vs
  ref+name). FORMAT OWNERSHIP MOVED 2026-08-17: the manifest FILE
  FORMAT itself is designed at [DD-13] (one unified format serving the
  manifest, the test carrier, and pcrec-bench's sets); this row keeps
  the compilation-unit + finder BUILD and its semantic rulings, which
  [DD-13] inherits as constraints
- [V-F] STATE:not-started — the SOURCE-SCAN TRANSFORMER (Frank, 2026-08-12,
  same discussion, same tier): scan a C program's sources for regex
  markers — `auto regex = rx/abc|def/` shaped — and rewrite them to
  references into a pcrec-compiled companion unit ([V-E]'s output format is
  the natural target). re2c/lex-shaped build tool. The dogfooding
  constraint IS the design constraint (Frank: the scanner uses a regex we
  compiled): the marker grammar must be REGULAR and unambiguous amid C
  strings/comments — chosen to be findable by the tool being sold, which
  makes the scanner both the demo and the spec. Do not start without a
  marker-grammar design note answering: escaping inside `rx/.../`, flags
  syntax, occurrences inside string literals and comments (skip or honor,
  and how a regular scanner distinguishes them)
- [V-G] STATE:not-started — USER-FACING REGEX TESTING (Frank, 2026-08-13,
  positioning discussion; boonies tier): expose the project's own dev
  testing machinery to a developer wishing to test THEIR regexes — the
  .rxt corpus format, the harness runner, and (where installed) the
  python-re / libpcre2 oracle differentials, as a `pcrec test`-shaped
  surface: write cases for your pattern, run them against your compiled
  matcher, optionally cross-check against the oracles. The machinery
  exists and is battle-proven (docs/testing.md); the work is packaging,
  scope (which harness features are user-grade vs dev-only), and docs —
  a docs/spec/ candidate when built. Nobody in the niche ships a regex
  TESTING story; this makes the verification story a user capability,
  not just an internal discipline. AMENDED 2026-08-13 (Frank, composition
  discussion): rides [V-E]'s named definitions — every named part of a
  manifest is independently compilable, so subpart testing is the .rxt
  harness pointed at each definition, per-part expectations in the same

- [V-H] STATE:not-started — DEBUG / TRACE EMISSION MODES (Frank,
  2026-08-13, same discussion; boonies tier): generation-time variants
  that aid understanding and debugging a matcher — a TRACING build
  (emitted matcher logs state transitions, positions, prefilter/skip
  entry/exit; a SEPARATE emitted variant per D18, zero cost in the normal
  emission — never a runtime flag in the hot loop), a VERBOSE compile
  mode narrating compilation decisions (prefilter chosen, skip states,
  trie factoring, caps hit), and the compile-time introspection DD-8
  already owes (--emit-ir / --emit-dot — that row stays the owner of
  those two surfaces; this row is the run-time half). Pairs with [V-G]:
  a failing user test plus a traced matcher is a debugging story no
  regex library offers. TRACE ENRICHMENT NOTE (Frank, 2026-08-21,
  thirty-fifth session — details/design TBD, stays boonies): revisit
  what --trace's emitted instrumentation SAYS — today it narrates
  engine mechanics (push/pop frames, state transitions); Frank wants it
  to also point at the position IN THE PATTERN being worked on (which
  construct — the `\n`, the quantifier — the engine is currently
  matching against, i.e. a pattern-offset/construct reference on trace
  events) and generally describe MORE than frame traffic. The emitter
  knows the pattern offset per emitted site at generation time, so this
  is plumbing generation-time knowledge into the trace strings — same
  family as the [M6-READ] legends (emitter-owned data rendered for a
  reader), which is the natural design starting point. If an enriched
  trace mode ever emits TABULAR output it adopts docs/spec/
  table_contract.md at birth

- [EMIT-SET] STATE:not-started (D88 2026-08-29: ONE artifact per emitted file, always — an emission set is a DIRECTORY of per-artifact files + a manifest header, composition is LINKING; no multi-pattern TU; R-VE-3's cross-artifact table sharing is link-level, deferred D77) — CONTROL OF WHAT IS EMITTED INTO THE C FILE (Frank, 2026-08-24, thirty-ninth session): a caller who only uses `match` does not need `search`; one who never reflects does not need `rx_info`; `main` folds into the same switch — `--emit -search,-rx_info,main` (a comma list of named EMISSION UNITS, `-` removes, bare name adds, defaults = today's artifact). The point beyond trimming: once emission is a SET of named units, VALUE-ADD units become drop-ins on the same switch — the results → static-struct copy helper ([V-I]), the compiled search/replace code ([M4-SUBST]), rx code-management helpers (versioning/identity stamps a consumer can query), the diagnostic variants ([V-H] `--trace`/[ENG-PGO] `--profile` are the same shape: a separate emitted variant selected at generation time), the caller-provided-buffer `_in` entries ([DD-14.FB] — a unit a caller may drop), a [LIB] library's shared regions. Design points at charter: the unit graph (dependencies — `main` needs `search`; `_in` needs the run struct; `rx_info` fields some units reference) resolved at generation with a clean refusal for an inconsistent set (D26 tier 3 wording); the [ABI-NS]/structural codegen checks become per-unit (a unit's presence/absence is a byte-level fact the identity gates assert — the default set byte-identical to today's artifact, the control); `--list-emit` prints the units and their defaults; the CLI/library API surface (`pcrec_options` gains the set; DD-3 versioning). D18's principle applied to the artifact's SURFACE: what is not emitted costs nothing, and a unit nobody asked for is not there to be wrong. Relations: `--emit-main` (today's only switch, becomes the `main` unit), OS-0's named-entry-point discipline, [DD-8] (`--emit-ir`/`--emit-dot` are OUTPUT formats, not units — keep them apart). Not started unprompted; PLANNED, its spine status deliberately open (Frank, 2026-08-24 ~22:0x) — the going-forward conversation at [DD-14]'s close decides.

- [PAT-LINT] STATE:not-started — OPTIONAL PATTERN ANALYSIS: an ahead-of-time
  check that names issues in a pattern the engines will accept but that
  behave badly (Frank, 2026-08-25, fortieth session, from the K34 ruling
  D74; PARKED — no queue position; details and design TBD). The charter
  case: ZERO-PROGRESS LEFT RECURSION — `(a|(?1)a)b` re-enters group 1 at
  the same subject position before consuming, so every non-matching
  subject is a runaway (pcrec gives up `frames`, PCRE2 concludes only by
  an implementation artefact, D74); the same language as `(a(?1)?)b` or
  `a+b`, which recurse after consuming and conclude in both engines.
  Rule 1: a call edge to group G reachable from G's own entry through a
  ZERO-MINIMUM-WIDTH prefix (the compiler already computes minimum widths
  — wave E's root-minw guard — so the analysis is a walk over facts it
  has). WARNING, NEVER A REFUSAL: the pattern is legal and matches on
  positive subjects; §3.3's deliberate same-position recursion with a
  base case is not a defect (the analysis says "may not terminate on
  non-matching input", not "wrong"). Other candidates for the same
  surface, each to be measured for a real occurrence before it is built:
  nested unbounded quantifiers over overlapping languages (the classic
  `(a+)+b` catastrophic-backtracking shape — relevant only where pcrec
  picks the VM engine; the DFA is immune, so the analysis must report
  ENGINE-AWARE, else it warns about a pattern that runs in linear time);
  empty-language callees (already refused at wave E — a diagnostic tier
  question whether it moves here); a dead group that never captures
  (wave G's elision list — informational). SURFACE TBD: a CLI flag
  (`--analyze` / `-W`) whose output is text, or a library entry; the
  D38 naming rules apply to any stamped result. Interacts with [V-G]
  (user-facing regex testing — the natural home if that lands first) and
  [V-H] (trace modes show the runaway; this names it before running).
  NOT QUEUED — parks behind the general work per the boonies discipline.
- [V-I] STATE:not-started — NAMED-RESULTS COPY HELPER (Frank, 2026-08-18,
  thirty-third session; LOW PRIORITY, boonies tier; details and design
  TBD): an emitted per-pattern helper that takes a search's results —
  in particular a captures-delivering search, most particularly one with
  NAMED groups — and copies them into a STATIC STRUCTURE keyed by the
  group names (the natural shape: an emitted `struct <prefix>_groups`
  with one span member per named group, plus a copier from the caps
  array). Generation-time is what makes it cheap: pcrec knows every name
  at emit time, and [M6.3]'s name grammar ([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]{0,127})
  makes every group name a lexically valid C identifier ALMOST for free
  — the TBD wrinkles already visible: C KEYWORDS are valid group names
  but invalid member names (`(?<int>...)`, `(?<return>...)` need a
  mangling rule), and D61's caps-layout promise plus rx_group_entry.slot
  are the substrate the copier reads through (slot-aware from birth, so
  a future ref-bearing row costs nothing). Interacts with [V-B]
  (bindings would love the same struct) and the D38 naming rules. NOT
  QUEUED — parks behind the general work per the boonies discipline

- [OPT-SETS] STATE:started (OPENED 2026-10-05 by Frank, ninetieth session, PLANNING ONLY — "no code now but it should be part of planning") — OPTION SETS: formalize NAMED SETS of compile options that toggle together, and how sets interact. Frank: "for simd and arch there will be sets of choices that should be toggled together — `simd`, `no-simd` and the various arch sets. They overlap. Plus the dial work is effectively 5 sets of options. Formalize the idea of option sets and how they interact; there may be other logical sets." Today's pieces this must unify rather than add beside (general-mechanisms rule): the per-optimization deny/force flags (`-fno-*`, deny bits through 46, `--list-axes`), `--tune=N`'s five pinned profiles (docs/spec/tuning.md §5, D103), `--engine`, encodings, `--features` modules, findings bundles, and the coming [MEMFN] families (ISA level v1/v3/v4 / armv8, SIMD on/off, the `--memfn-deny=` family proposal, isa_selection.md/isa_evaluation.md/integration.md). Design questions: what a set IS (a named bundle of option assignments vs a predicate over the option space); membership and OVERLAP (a flag in several sets); composition and precedence (set vs set, set vs explicit flag, file-wins, `.rxt` config blocks); conflicts refused by name, never resolved silently; how a set is STAMPED in the artifact and in rx_info; how test-axes, the identity gates and the bench treat a set as an axis; and whether the dial's five positions become five sets. First step: a design note (lane optsets), then a light panel; no build until a consumer ([MEMFN] R4's ISA/SIMD rows) needs it (D77).

Named generally on purpose ("pcrec-simd is too specific"); working name `memory-functions`. Frank's sequencing: (R1) REQUIREMENTS FIRST — needs derived from pcrec's search-site inventory (docs/design/compare_stack.md §2), D91's two budgets, [OPT-SIMD]'s ISA/dispatch notes, studies/simd1, the bench's class-shape census, AND a binding-form criterion: when a kernel may be a FUNCTION CALL (libc-style, PLT/ifunc, ~3.5 ns/call measured by lane k82cost), an INLINE call (static inline / header-only), or INJECTED code (emitted text specialized per pattern) — the K82 short-call term (~10 ns pair-arm floor, two memchr calls) is the motivating case, "it shouldn't have this 10ns overhead"; (R2) SURVEY existing open-source implementations against R1 (adopt / borrow ideas / build) — "it doesn't make sense to blow a bunch of time reinventing the wheel; at a minimum get ideas"; (R3) findings to Frank, who decides the way forward (adopt, fork, or a NEW SEPARATE REPO — the scope mandate is extended only on that ruling). Deliverables live in docs/design/memfn/ until then. If built, get it underway early so [OPT-SIMD] is not waiting. Does NOT relax D91: nothing here touches pcrec's emission. OWED: Linux/x86 timing of the survey harness with R1 §2.6's run. Next: R3 (findings to Frank). **R1d DELIVERED 2026-10-04 (lane memfnmap): docs/design/memfn/integration.md** — Frank: integrate, SIMD variants slot into pcrec's decision tables. Table inventory T1-T7 (+7 table-less scan sites); SIMD goes into ONE new nested scan-form table SCAN_ROWS (rows loopfree / vec-verify / vec / libc-memchr / leapfrog / swar / table-walk; today's text = byte-identical promotion), not vector twins of every dfa_pfs row; boundary recommendation (c): kit = per-ISA injectable primitives + a composition generator + CLI/reference functions, pcrec keeps selection/operands/fusion/injection; Q12-Q17 open to Frank; build order R3-R4g with D77 triggers in its §6. Side finding filed as K84. Hand-twin measurement (lane memftwin) in flight. **R1d DELIVERED 2026-10-04 (lane memftwin): `docs/design/memfn/twins.md`** — the D77 tailored-vs-generic measurement, hand twins in `docs/design/memfn/probes/twins/` (Mac, directional): T-A a per-shape set classifier saves ~1,000 ns per 64 KiB miss over the generic nibble lookup for ranges/one-bit cubes, ~600-700 for eq2/two-bit cube/nibble-unique, 0 for eq3, LOSES for `\w`; on dense text the find-first RESTART (~7.5 ns a hit) dominates every classifier (an iterate-in-place form is the lever); on SSE2-only x86 the shape forms are the only vector forms. T-B a fused pair-filter scan+verify cuts today's emitted K82 run gate ~7x on all three cells (union-select 16,829 -> 2,408 ns per 64 KiB; mod-i sweep 40,594 -> 5,331), the cost being the per-scan-byte stop (~10 ns). Each kit row declares an allowlist of the site fields it honours. One shared gate declines everything else, so a contract field added later is declined by default rather than silently served (K96's shape: R4c's ofsskip arm served sites with a floor or a non-n miss it could not honour). Decisions become visible: which row was chosen, and why the others declined. Each row has a reach floor. Flow: read-only audit (kit, sonnet, running 2026-10-07) → light design → D6 panel → build. ZERO-MOVER for pcrec. If the design needs a pcrec-side explain/trace hook, it arrives as a kit request. Sibling of a family: the kit's row selection is a first-match table (D152), and this adds the per-row admission column it lacked. **Frank's constraint (2026-10-07, given in the kit session):** decisions move into memfn in the future, so the explain/visibility record is KIT-OWNED (a per-site decision record behind a kit API). Its shape is chosen so that [START-TABLE] C1's PCREC_CAND_TRACE site-key records can MIGRATE into it when the planner moves into the kit. So [START-TABLE] C2-C7 should keep the trace records in the literal-site-key form, ready to migrate. No pcrec request (R-6) is expected now. **Q-ROW-1 RULED by Frank (2026-10-07, in the kit session):** (1) NO SILENT DEFAULTS. Every value a kit row reads is stated with a named token (e.g. MF_MISS_N), and an unstated value is a loud refusal. This brings a later ZERO-MOVER pcrec request (R-6): pcrec's hook builders state MF_MISS_N, "0", etc. explicitly. (2) SNAPSHOT. Each delegated site evaluates each caller hook ONCE into a reserved `mf_` local, parenthesized (closing the precedence, double-evaluation, macro, mid-site-mutation and capture hazards). This is a pcrec ABI EVENT at every delegated site (G1 at both layers; deny `--memfn=no-snapshot`). It sequences through the manager among the START-TABLE bumps, with a bench inbox notice at landing. ROWCON's rev 2 hosts C2's cand_rows[] on paper with no special cases; analyses[] and [POSS-CTX-TABLE] are declared not hosted (not first-match). **Rest RULED by Frank (2026-10-07):** Q-ROW-4 AGREE: D146's charter widens, the kit hosts the general first-match table engine as an MF_NS utility, and pcrec tables may adopt it per table as an offer after C7. Q-ROW-6 AGREE: the snapshot has NO public deny; G1 compares against the pre-snapshot commit (a D144 item-4 exception for correctness changes). This SUPERSEDES the `--memfn=no-snapshot` deny above. Q-ROW-1/5 NARROWED: "a row may USE only a value the caller STATED"; unused fields are wildcards. So R-6 shrinks to stating only what the chosen rows use (e.g. MF_MISS_N on valued handoffs); the kit sends the list after its rev 3. Rev 3's pcrec-facing fixes: routes==0 is a definition error (on main it means DFA-only), a typed test thunk keeps pcrec's bool predicates as-is, an on_none hook instead of abort-on-none, and no second route carrier. **SCOPE NARROWED by Frank (2026-10-07): "I don't want this to turn into a solution without problem scenario."** BUILD NOW only the K96 generalization: per-row allowlists of USED fields, one shared decline gate, visible decline reasons in the kit trace, per-row reach floors, and the stated-value rule (R-6 stays small). HELD: (a) the general table engine; Q-ROW-4 stays ruled, but it builds only when a real table wants to adopt it, and the cand_rows[] mapping stays as design evidence only. (b) SNAPSHOT (T3b); its trigger is a census of pcrec's actual hook expressions showing a non-primary/side-effecting/precedence-fragile hook, or a migration that introduces one, otherwise it stays filed with the census as its evidence. Next panel: a light re-check of the narrowed gate. **HOOK CENSUS (kit, 2026-10-07): NO RISKY HOOK.** Every value hook pcrec passes (s, n, lo, result, result_decl, indent) is a bare identifier, on_miss is always `return 0;`, and fn/table names are plain identifiers. SNAPSHOT stays FILED with memfn's probes/rowcon/hook_census.md as its evidence, and its trigger is re-checked at every migration step that adds or changes a hook. ROWCON brings NO abi event. M4-hosted, boonies-queued (Frank's queue discipline places these after the

- [ENG-THIN] STATE:not-started (under [OPTLOOP] candidates per D137) — MRL CLAMP GATING/THINNING (Frank,
  2026-08-17, twenty-ninth session, ruled at the [M4.6d] accept-as-is
  decision — D51 addendum has the accepted +8% residual this row exists
  to shrink): decide per PATTERN — or per SITE — whether the MRL clamp
  is emitted at all. SOUNDNESS-FREE BY CONSTRUCTION: with or without
  the clamp the VM returns identical answers (the 202,458-cell [M4.6d]
  differential is the demonstration); gating errors cost budget
  exposure or overhead, never wrong answers, so this is pure
  measure-then-implement optimization. TWO HALVES, Frank's two
  questions verbatim: (1) ANALYTIC, compile-time — the k23_design.md
  closed-form step law (validated exact out of sample) gives a
  worst-case unclamped step bound per pattern; clamp only where that
  bound exceeds a threshold against the step budget. Boolean
  is-it-ambiguous gating is NOT sufficient — the +8% shape
  ([a-z]{2,4}){2,8}b IS ambiguous-class; the gate must be
  quantitative. Conservative generalization of the law is this row's
  engineering; when uncertain, KEEP the clamp (wrong-keep costs <=8%
  on a bad shape, wrong-skip costs an honest refusal, neither is
  unsound). Per-site refinement: clamp only quantifiers participating
  in nests whose bound exceeds threshold. (2) EXEMPLAR-STATISTICAL —
  owned by [ENG-PGO], linked: given representative subjects ([DD-13]'s
  exemplar file references are the designed carrier), tune the COST
  side where (1) says the clamp is not strictly needed; exemplar
  stats must never override the safety side (tail inputs are exactly
  what exemplars do not show; DD-2's budget refusal stays the
  backstop). If a caller wants both variants, OS-0's named entry
  points are the D18-compliant shape — no hot-loop dispatch.
  REGRESSION TRIPWIRES already in place: run_mrl_tests.sh §1b (the
  8-step counter-rung acceptance cell) and
  tests/base/d27_k23_ambiguous_decomposition.rxt (89 blinded cells) —
  over-thinning that reintroduces a K23-class blowup fails both,
  loudly. Schedule: boonies-queued with a natural measurement window
  alongside [M4.6e]'s VM perf work.
- [ENG-PGO] STATE:not-started (under [OPTLOOP] candidates per D137) — (CROSS-NOTE 2026-08-29 19:4x, Frank on [OPT-4]'s default: the count-collapse decision "sounds like the kind of thing that analyzing the exemplar file with a profiling version of the pattern would answer" — a second PGO-informed selection point beside [OPT-A]'s rarest byte: the findings file's subject profile (candidate-start density, run lengths at the repeat) decides exact vs collapsed per pattern; the static knee-128 stays the profile-less default.) (CROSS-NOTE 2026-08-29, Frank: the FINDINGS FILE is an .rxt — a `freq <name>` DATA block (a byte-frequency table) that a target's config references; docs/design/dd13_format/frank_inputs.md 2026-08-29, usecases_and_outline.md §6.5; blocks on [DD-13b] wave 2/3.) D83 (2026-08-28) RULES THE SHAPE: exemplar analysis runs OUTSIDE pcrec, once per file, delivering a FINDINGS FILE pcrec accepts; FILE-GENERAL findings (byte frequencies etc., pattern-independent, one exemplar serves many patterns) and PATTERN-SPECIFIC findings (a statistics-recording special compilation, built only for a specific question, later) are two result files and two builds. The bench's [B11.1] presence counts are the first exhibit. FIRST CONCRETE QUESTION for the profile attachment (Frank, 2026-08-25 ~21:2x, at the [OPT-1] two-tier ruling): the tier design is a BET that the fast tier holds the overwhelming majority of real calls; a `--profile` variant run over an EXEMPLAR subject file would put a number on it — the escalation count (FRAMES → deep tier) per call, per pattern, per subject class — and is the trigger D79 names, made concrete: build the profiler when the loop needs this number, and this is the number. CHEAP FIRST STEP (srTier, 2026-08-25): the `-DRX_TEST_TIER_HOOK` build of a tiered artifact already counts escalations per call — a driver over an exemplar subject file yields the escalation rate WITHOUT the profiler; charter that as [OPT-1]'s follow-up measurement before any profiler design. (Cross-note continues.) — CROSS-NOTE (Frank, 2026-08-24, thirty-ninth session, direction not decision): the `--profile` emission mode below gains a SECOND CUSTOMER — the post-spine pcrec-bench OPTIMIZATION LOOP (journal 2026-08-24 direction note): a PROFILE ATTACHMENT on a compiled pattern that says where the matcher spends its time BY SECTION (prefilter scan, skip loop, VM attempt, per rung) and, where possible, in smaller increments, run over a variety of scenarios, to guide optimization PRIORITY — `--trace` is the debugging tool, the profile is the optimization tool. Design starting points (manager, same day): time attribution needs cycle deltas (`rdtsc`/`clock_gettime`) at the section boundaries the emitter already names, not counts alone; the computed-goto labels are natural fine increments (count + cycles per label = a per-construct profile with no `perf`/symbolizer), and can carry the pattern offset the [V-H] trace-enrichment note wants; the profile struct is read through an `rx_info`-shaped accessor; the artifact stamps that it is the profile variant (identity gate: the default artifact is byte-identical across the axis). Same [V-H] namespace as D71.1's counter axis — a second diagnostic axis is the trigger D71 names for designing the GROUP rather than the flag. PROFILE-GUIDED GENERATION (Frank,
  2026-08-16, twenty-seventh session — promoted from the parenthetical
  inside [SIMD-META] to its own row at his ask; the SIMD-META exemplar-axis
  mention now points here). TWO NAMED CUSTOMERS as of 2026-08-17 (D53
  addendum): (1) [ENG-THIN]'s clamp-cost tuning; (2) HYBRID-VS-VM-ONLY
  ENGINE SELECTION per pattern — D53 killed the runtime length branch
  (the deciding variable is match OFFSET, unknowable pre-search), but
  the offset DISTRIBUTION is a static workload fact an exemplar file
  exposes; preferred form is pure PGO (build both variants, run the
  exemplars, keep the measured winner — absorbing per-attempt-cost
  effects no distribution model sees), with OS-0 named entry points
  for mixed/unknown workloads. The idea: compile a pattern exactly as today,
  but with an opt-in `--profile` emission mode whose matcher TRACKS HOW IT
  RAN on the user's exemplar inputs — a PROFILE, not a trace: counters at
  the observation points the emitter already names (D46 stamp points,
  DD-8's tracer sites; same structural constraint — derived from the
  structure the emitter walks, never a parallel description). Stats out

  iteration-count histograms per quantifier, match/fail mix, subject byte
  frequencies), then a RECOMPILE-WITH-STATS channel feeds them back as
  generation inputs. SCALAR CUSTOMERS EXIST — this row is not
  SIMD-contingent: alternation/trie arm ordering by hit frequency;
  prefilter literal choice by measured-rarest byte (scalar memchr benefits
  identically to any SIMD scanner); the §8.1 capture-walk sink knob

  selection; step-budget sizing near K23-style boundaries. RELATION TO

  adaptive choices keep getting deferred because an axis must earn itself
  with a measurement — PGO is the EARNING MECHANISM, the standing vehicle
  for every deferred adaptivity question ([ENG-CLAMP], adaptive K, arm
